Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, nice to meet you, Doc. Honestly, what finally got me in here is that over these past few months, my mind just keeps jumping all over the place. Like, I'm in the cab, and every single time the CB chirps, I'm completely derailed. 

Speaking of the CB, the chatter lately has been just insane. I was hauling down I-80 the other day and everyone was going on and on about this backup near Omaha, and I swear, the way people drive through construction zones now is just a death wish. Nobody respects the rigs anymore, you know? They just cut right in front of your bumper like you can stop eighty thousand pounds on a dime. 

But anyway... what was I saying? Right, so my brain just latches onto that stuff, or every single fuel price sign I pass—which, don't even get me started on diesel prices right now, it's absolute robbery. I don't know how the independent guys are even keeping their trucks running. But because my head is just bouncing around with all these new ideas and every little distraction, I'm dropping the ball. I'm missing details I normally never, ever miss. I'll completely overlook a client’s delivery note, or I'll totally forget who I promised to call back. I've been driving for a long time and I don't make rookie mistakes like that, so... yeah, I figured I better come talk to somebody.

Well, yeah, actually. For months now, I've had these stretches where I'm just... I don't know, too fired up. Like I'm on top of the world and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what. Which is a nice feeling, I guess. Reminds me of when I first started driving, back in the late nineties. Man, the rigs were different then. Simpler. You didn't have all these computers telling you when to shift or tracking your every move. It was just you, the diesel, and the open road. I used to run this old Peterbilt, beautiful truck, cherry red... 

But anyway, yeah, I get really upbeat. Or, on the flip side, I get super irritable. I'll be home off a run, and I'm just snapping over the smallest things. Like, my neighbor’s dog was barking the other day, and normally I love dogs—I had a Golden Retriever named Buster for twelve years, best dog you could ever ask for, he used to ride in the cab with me sometimes—but this barking just set me off. Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. 

Usually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... 

But yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.

I swear, if they broke down outside of Barstow in the middle of July, they'd just sit in the cab and cry. Back when I started, you had to know how to actually fix your own rig. I remember this one time my radiator blew on Route 66, right near this old diner that made the absolute best cherry pie you've ever had in your life. The waitress there—I think her name was Betty, or maybe Dot?—she gave me a slice on the house while I waited for parts to come in from Flagstaff. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just get so antsy if I sit still for too long. I completely took apart the lawnmower last week because the blades were getting dull, and you know, we've got that Bermuda grass in the front yard. If you don't stay on top of it, it just completely takes over the flower beds, and my wife just spent a fortune planting all these petunias... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the energy. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.

I've been driving for a long time and I don't make rookie mistakes like that, so... yeah, I figured I better come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. For months now, I've had these stretches where I'm just... I don't know, too fired up. Like I'm on top of the world and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what. Which is a nice feeling, I guess. Reminds me of when I first started driving, back in the late nineties. Man, the rigs were different then. Simpler. You didn't have all these computers telling you when to shift or tracking your every move. It was just you, the diesel, and the open road. I used to run this old Peterbilt, beautiful truck, cherry red... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I get really upbeat. Or, on the flip side, I get super irritable. I'll be home off a run, and I'm just snapping over the smallest things. Like, my neighbor’s dog was barking the other day, and normally I love dogs—I had a Golden Retriever named Buster for twelve years, best dog you could ever ask for, he used to ride in the cab with me sometimes—but this barking just set me off. Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you were so full of energy or ideas that you were doing things much more intensely than usual, like taking on big projects, talking more than usual, or feeling like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, for sure. I just keep finding extra jobs for myself lately. Like, I’ll be up rewriting all my route plans to shave off a few miles, or I'm calling to check on the new drivers way after hours. And honestly, some of these new kids they’re hiring now... they don't know a wrench from a steering wheel. I swear, if they broke down outside of Barstow in the middle of July, they'd just sit in the cab and cry. Back when I started, you had to know how to actually fix your own rig. I remember this one time my radiator blew on Route 66, right near this old diner that made the absolute best cherry pie you've ever had in your life. The waitress there—I think her name was Betty, or maybe Dot?—she gave me a slice on the house while I waited for parts to come in from Flagstaff. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just get so antsy if I sit still for too long. If I try to watch TV, my skin just crawls. So if I'm not doing truck stuff, I'm fixing every little thing around the house. I completely took apart the lawnmower last week because the blades were getting dull, and you know, we've got that Bermuda grass in the front yard. If you don't stay on top of it, it just completely takes over the flower beds, and my wife just spent a fortune planting all these petunias... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the energy. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... \n\nBut yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... 

But yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.

Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. 

But man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... 

Wait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.

Right, so my brain just latches onto that stuff, or every single fuel price sign I pass—which, don't even get me started on diesel prices right now, it's absolute robbery. I don't know how the independent guys are even keeping their trucks running. But because my head is just bouncing around with all these new ideas and every little distraction, I'm dropping the ball. I'm missing details I normally never, ever miss. I'll completely overlook a client’s delivery note, or I'll totally forget who I promised to call back. I've been driving for a long time and I don't make rookie mistakes like that, so... yeah, I figured I better come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. For months now, I've had these stretches where I'm just... I don't know, too fired up. Like I'm on top of the world and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what. Which is a nice feeling, I guess. Reminds me of when I first started driving, back in the late nineties. Man, the rigs were different then. Simpler. You didn't have all these computers telling you when to shift or tracking your every move. It was just you, the diesel, and the open road. I used to run this old Peterbilt, beautiful truck, cherry red... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I get really upbeat. Or, on the flip side, I get super irritable. I'll be home off a run, and I'm just snapping over the smallest things. Like, my neighbor’s dog was barking the other day, and normally I love dogs—I had a Golden Retriever named Buster for twelve years, best dog you could ever ask for, he used to ride in the cab with me sometimes—but this barking just set me off. Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you were so full of energy or ideas that you were doing things much more intensely than usual, like taking on big projects, talking more than usual, or feeling like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, for sure. I just keep finding extra jobs for myself lately. Like, I’ll be up rewriting all my route plans to shave off a few miles, or I'm calling to check on the new drivers way after hours. And honestly, some of these new kids they’re hiring now... they don't know a wrench from a steering wheel. I swear, if they broke down outside of Barstow in the middle of July, they'd just sit in the cab and cry. Back when I started, you had to know how to actually fix your own rig. I remember this one time my radiator blew on Route 66, right near this old diner that made the absolute best cherry pie you've ever had in your life. The waitress there—I think her name was Betty, or maybe Dot?—she gave me a slice on the house while I waited for parts to come in from Flagstaff. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just get so antsy if I sit still for too long. If I try to watch TV, my skin just crawls. So if I'm not doing truck stuff, I'm fixing every little thing around the house. I completely took apart the lawnmower last week because the blades were getting dull, and you know, we've got that Bermuda grass in the front yard. If you don't stay on top of it, it just completely takes over the flower beds, and my wife just spent a fortune planting all these petunias... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the energy. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... \n\nBut yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Do they go on for days at a time, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Your goal is to answer the doctor's question naturally, using the\ninformation you are given.\n\nAnswer with the [Targeted Info], told in the way [Conversation Style] describes.\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Do they go on for days at a time, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave there been any specific times during these seven months when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a whole week?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 50 / driver_sales_worker_or_truck_driver / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, I don't know about a specific week or anything like that. Like I said, it's just been this whole stretch of time. I haven't really been tracking it week by week, you know? Just trying to get through the days. 

Speaking of getting through the days, I had to drive a route down to El Paso last month. Longest haul I've done in a while. The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Bought a cheap acoustic from a pawn shop, but my fingers are just too thick for the strings. Kept muting the chords. My buddy Dave, though, he could play anything with strings. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... 

But yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. It's just all kind of blended together since my daughter moved back.

Well, like I keep trying to explain, it's not really just a week here or a week there in the past. It’s right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what happens, or I’m just snapping at everyone over the smallest little things around the house and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way, you know? I'm usually pretty level-headed. 

Speaking of the yard, I finally got that John Deere running again yesterday. Turns out it wasn't just the carburetor flooding, the fuel line was cracked right near the filter. Had to drive all the way down to the hardware store on 4th Street to get a replacement hose. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. Got a practice up in Boise... 

But yeah, I haven't really had separate weeks in the past like you're asking. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.

And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. Like I said, it's just been this whole stretch of time. I haven't really been tracking it week by week, you know? Just trying to get through the days. \n\nSpeaking of getting through the days, I had to drive a route down to El Paso last month. Longest haul I've done in a while. The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Bought a cheap acoustic from a pawn shop, but my fingers are just too thick for the strings. Kept muting the chords. My buddy Dave, though, he could play anything with strings. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. It's just all kind of blended together since my daughter moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any distinct times in the past when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a full week at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I keep trying to explain, it's not really just a week here or a week there in the past. It’s right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what happens, or I’m just snapping at everyone over the smallest little things around the house and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way, you know? I'm usually pretty level-headed. \n\nSpeaking of the yard, I finally got that John Deere running again yesterday. Turns out it wasn't just the carburetor flooding, the fuel line was cracked right near the filter. Had to drive all the way down to the hardware store on 4th Street to get a replacement hose. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. Got a practice up in Boise... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really had separate weeks in the past like you're asking. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.\n\n[Last Question]\nCan you think back and tell me if there were any specific times when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a whole week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Male / 50 / driver_sales_worker_or_truck_driver / high_school\n- severity: Mild\n\n[Targeted Info]\nname: Elevated_Irritable_Mood\nmanifestation: \"For months now I’ve been either too fired up, like everything is going to work out no matter what, or I’m snapping over small stuff at home and in the yard, which isn’t my usual way.\"\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, I feel like a broken record here. Like I've been trying to tell you, it's not just one specific week in the past that I can point to. It's right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m snapping at everyone over the smallest little things at home and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way. 

But speaking of broken records, my daughter actually brought a real record player with her when she moved back in. Vinyl, she calls it. Takes up half the living room console. She's got all these old albums she bought at thrift stores... Fleetwood Mac, The Eagles. I used to have Hotel California on 8-track in my first rig. It was a Peterbilt 359, classic long hood. Man, I put a million miles on that truck. The sleeper cab was so small you couldn't even sit up straight in it, but she rode like an absolute dream on the interstate. I had this custom shifter knob made out of a real eight-ball that I won in a pool tournament down in Amarillo. The guy I beat was so mad he broke his cue over his knee right there in the bar... 

Wait, what was the question again? Did you want to know about my daughter moving back in?

Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you were so full of energy or ideas that you were doing things much more intensely than usual, like taking on big projects, talking more than usual, or feeling like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, for sure. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... \n\nBut yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Do they go on for days at a time, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any specific times during these seven months when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a whole week?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about a specific week or anything like that. Like I said, it's just been this whole stretch of time. I haven't really been tracking it week by week, you know? Just trying to get through the days. \n\nSpeaking of getting through the days, I had to drive a route down to El Paso last month. Longest haul I've done in a while. The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Bought a cheap acoustic from a pawn shop, but my fingers are just too thick for the strings. Kept muting the chords. My buddy Dave, though, he could play anything with strings. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were any specific times when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a whole week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like a broken record here. Look, Doc, I don't mean to be difficult, but I really don't know how else to say it. I'm not talking about some time way back when. I'm talking about right now, these past months. It's just been this constant thing where I'm either completely fired up, like I'm invincible and everything's gonna be just fine no matter what, or I'm biting everyone's head off over the dumbest little things around the house or out working in the yard. Said it looked like someone was just waiting to watch a game. Speaking of softball, my daughter used to play. She was a pitcher, had a mean fastball. We used to practice in the backyard until it got so dark we couldn't see the ball anymore. We went through so many softballs, losing them in the neighbor's hedges...

Right, so my brain just latches onto that stuff, or every single fuel price sign I pass—which, don't even get me started on diesel prices right now, it's absolute robbery. I don't know how the independent guys are even keeping their trucks running. But because my head is just bouncing around with all these new ideas and every little distraction, I'm dropping the ball. I'm missing details I normally never, ever miss. I'll completely overlook a client’s delivery note, or I'll totally forget who I promised to call back. I've been driving for a long time and I don't make rookie mistakes like that, so... yeah, I figured I better come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. For months now, I've had these stretches where I'm just... I don't know, too fired up. Like I'm on top of the world and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what. Which is a nice feeling, I guess. Reminds me of when I first started driving, back in the late nineties. Man, the rigs were different then. Simpler. You didn't have all these computers telling you when to shift or tracking your every move. It was just you, the diesel, and the open road. I used to run this old Peterbilt, beautiful truck, cherry red... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I get really upbeat. Or, on the flip side, I get super irritable. I'll be home off a run, and I'm just snapping over the smallest things. Like, my neighbor’s dog was barking the other day, and normally I love dogs—I had a Golden Retriever named Buster for twelve years, best dog you could ever ask for, he used to ride in the cab with me sometimes—but this barking just set me off. Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you were so full of energy or ideas that you were doing things much more intensely than usual, like taking on big projects, talking more than usual, or feeling like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, for sure. I just keep finding extra jobs for myself lately. Like, I’ll be up rewriting all my route plans to shave off a few miles, or I'm calling to check on the new drivers way after hours. And honestly, some of these new kids they’re hiring now... they don't know a wrench from a steering wheel. I swear, if they broke down outside of Barstow in the middle of July, they'd just sit in the cab and cry. Back when I started, you had to know how to actually fix your own rig. I remember this one time my radiator blew on Route 66, right near this old diner that made the absolute best cherry pie you've ever had in your life. The waitress there—I think her name was Betty, or maybe Dot?—she gave me a slice on the house while I waited for parts to come in from Flagstaff. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just get so antsy if I sit still for too long. If I try to watch TV, my skin just crawls. So if I'm not doing truck stuff, I'm fixing every little thing around the house. I completely took apart the lawnmower last week because the blades were getting dull, and you know, we've got that Bermuda grass in the front yard. If you don't stay on top of it, it just completely takes over the flower beds, and my wife just spent a fortune planting all these petunias... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the energy. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... \n\nBut yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Do they go on for days at a time, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any specific times during these seven months when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a whole week?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about a specific week or anything like that. Like I said, it's just been this whole stretch of time. I haven't really been tracking it week by week, you know? Just trying to get through the days. \n\nSpeaking of getting through the days, I had to drive a route down to El Paso last month. Longest haul I've done in a while. The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Bought a cheap acoustic from a pawn shop, but my fingers are just too thick for the strings. Kept muting the chords. My buddy Dave, though, he could play anything with strings. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. It's just all kind of blended together since my daughter moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any distinct times in the past when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a full week at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I keep trying to explain, it's not really just a week here or a week there in the past. It’s right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what happens, or I’m just snapping at everyone over the smallest little things around the house and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way, you know? I'm usually pretty level-headed. \n\nSpeaking of the yard, I finally got that John Deere running again yesterday. Turns out it wasn't just the carburetor flooding, the fuel line was cracked right near the filter. Had to drive all the way down to the hardware store on 4th Street to get a replacement hose. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. Got a practice up in Boise... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really had separate weeks in the past like you're asking. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were any specific times when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a whole week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like a broken record here. Like I've been trying to tell you, it's not just one specific week in the past that I can point to. It's right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m snapping at everyone over the smallest little things at home and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way. \n\nBut speaking of broken records, my daughter actually brought a real record player with her when she moved back in. Vinyl, she calls it. Takes up half the living room console. She's got all these old albums she bought at thrift stores... Fleetwood Mac, The Eagles. I used to have Hotel California on 8-track in my first rig. It was a Peterbilt 359, classic long hood. Man, I put a million miles on that truck. The sleeper cab was so small you couldn't even sit up straight in it, but she rode like an absolute dream on the interstate. I had this custom shifter knob made out of a real eight-ball that I won in a pool tournament down in Amarillo. The guy I beat was so mad he broke his cue over his knee right there in the bar... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Did you want to know about my daughter moving back in?\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time before these past seven months when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a full week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I don't mean to be difficult, but I really don't know how else to say it. I'm not talking about some time way back when. I'm talking about right now, these past months. It's just been this constant thing where I'm either completely fired up, like I'm invincible and everything's gonna be just fine no matter what, or I'm biting everyone's head off over the dumbest little things around the house or out working in the yard. And that's just not me. I'm not a guy who flies off the handle. \n\nSpeaking of handles, I actually need to fix the handle on my favorite shovel. The wood's starting to splinter near the grip. I’ve had that shovel for fifteen years. Bought it at a garage sale from a guy who used to work for the city parks department. He had all kinds of crazy stories about what they’d find in the parks after dark. He told me once they found a whole living room set—couch, TV, coffee table, the works—just set up right in the middle of the softball field at Memorial Park. Said it looked like someone was just waiting to watch a game. Speaking of softball, my daughter used to play. She was a pitcher, had a mean fastball. We used to practice in the backyard until it got so dark we couldn't see the ball anymore. We went through so many softballs, losing them in the neighbor's hedges... \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, it's just been these past months of feeling either way too up or just plain irritable. Not some specific week in the past.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

Doc, I swear we're going in circles here. I really can't think of any time before this recent stretch. It's just been these last seven months or so where I'm either feeling ten feet tall and bulletproof or ready to bite somebody's head off over nothing. Before that? But anyway, no. Before these last few months, I don't remember any weeks where I was acting all crazy energized or irritable like you're talking about. Just this current stretch.

He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... But yeah, no, nobody's really said anything about me acting intense or spending too much or whatever.\n\nDoctor: How long do these periods of feeling very upbeat, energized, or irritable usually last? Do they go on for days at a time, or longer?\n\nPatient: Well, it's been going on for a while now. I'd say about seven months. It all started right around the time my daughter moved back home. She had this apartment up in Seattle, but the rent was just astronomical, and she couldn't find a decent job after she finished her degree. It's tough out there for young people right now, you know? Not like when I was her age. When I got out of high school, I just walked down to the local distribution center, shook the manager's hand, and I was driving a forklift by Monday. His name was Mr. Henderson. Old guy, always wore these bright red suspenders and smoked those cheap cigars that smelled like burning tires. He was tough, but fair. If you messed up, he'd chew you out, but he'd also be the first one to buy you a beer on Friday after your shift. We used to go to this place called The Rusty Anchor. They had the best dartboards in town, real bristle boards, not those cheap electronic ones where the darts bounce right out. I used to be pretty good at darts, actually. Won a tournament there back in '94... \n\nBut yeah, since she moved back, I've just been trying to keep everything steady, you know? Make sure she's got what she needs, keep the house running, keep working. It's just been a solid seven months of feeling like this, just going and going.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods where your mood was unusually high, irritable, or energized, lasting for at least a full week, where you were acting differently enough that someone who knows you well might have noticed a change in your usual behavior?\n\nPatient: Well, like I was saying, it's been months now. And yeah, I guess during this whole time I’ve been either way too fired up, like I just know everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. Which really isn’t my usual way, I'm usually a pretty easygoing guy. \n\nBut man, the yard work lately... I was out there trying to fix my lawnmower last week because the carburetor keeps flooding. It's a John Deere, you'd think it would hold up better, but they just don't make small engines like they used to. Used to be you could take a wrench to anything and fix it yourself. My dad taught me how to rebuild a carburetor when I was twelve. We had this old Chevy pickup, a '72 C10, stepside bed, beautiful truck. It was baby blue. He bought it off a guy who used it to haul firewood up in the mountains. We spent every weekend that summer in the garage, smelling like grease and gasoline. My mom hated it, she'd yell at us for tracking oil onto the linoleum kitchen floor. She had that pattern, you know the one, with the little yellow and orange flowers? God, she loved that floor. Scrubbed it on her hands and knees every Sunday morning before church... \n\nWait, what were we talking about? Oh, right, my mood. But yeah, for months it's just been either feeling totally unstoppable or getting mad over absolutely nothing.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any specific times during these seven months when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a whole week?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't know about a specific week or anything like that. Like I said, it's just been this whole stretch of time. I haven't really been tracking it week by week, you know? Just trying to get through the days. \n\nSpeaking of getting through the days, I had to drive a route down to El Paso last month. Longest haul I've done in a while. The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. It's just all kind of blended together since my daughter moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any distinct times in the past when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a full week at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I keep trying to explain, it's not really just a week here or a week there in the past. It’s right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what happens, or I’m just snapping at everyone over the smallest little things around the house and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way, you know? I'm usually pretty level-headed. \n\nSpeaking of the yard, I finally got that John Deere running again yesterday. Turns out it wasn't just the carburetor flooding, the fuel line was cracked right near the filter. Had to drive all the way down to the hardware store on 4th Street to get a replacement hose. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were any specific times when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a whole week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like a broken record here. It was a Peterbilt 359, classic long hood. Man, I put a million miles on that truck. The sleeper cab was so small you couldn't even sit up straight in it, but she rode like an absolute dream on the interstate. He had all kinds of crazy stories about what they’d find in the parks after dark. Oh, right. Yeah, it's just been these past months of feeling either way too up or just plain irritable. Not some specific week in the past.\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, can you recall any times in your life when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a full week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I swear we're going in circles here. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Doc, I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall here. I keep telling you, yes! But it's not just one week. For months now, I've been completely wired, just way too fired up. Like I'm invincible and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what happens.
