I had to order the part from this guy in Nebraska off the internet. Took three weeks to get here. The mail system is just a joke these days. I remember when my uncle worked for the post office back in the eighties. He drove one of those little white trucks with the steering wheel on the wrong side. He used to let me ride in the passenger side—well, the left side—when he did his rural route on Saturdays. We'd listen to the AM radio, mostly farm reports and old country music. George Jones, Merle Haggard. You don't hear music like that anymore. Now it's all just computers making noises... 

But yeah, to answer your question, it's been months of feeling like this, not just a week. Too up or too irritable. Just not myself.

Doc, I... I don't know how many different ways I can say this. I feel like a broken record. Yes, my mood has been totally out of whack, but it's not some specific week in the past. For months now, I’ve been either way too fired up, like I'm completely unstoppable and everything is going to work out no matter what, or I’m just snapping at everybody over the smallest little things around the house or out in the yard. You know, speaking of rolling, I had a blowout on my rig about three weeks ago on I-80. Rear passenger side tire just shredded. Sounded like a shotgun going off. Scared the living daylights out of this guy in a little silver Honda next to me. He swerved so hard I thought he was going into the ditch. I managed to limp it over to the shoulder, but let me tell you, changing a tire on a semi on the side of the interstate with semis blowing past you at seventy miles an hour... it's enough to rattle your teeth. The wind coming off those trucks just hits you like a physical wall. My dad used to drive a rig back in the seventies, long before all the electronic logs and GPS stuff. He used to keep a little notebook on the dash and write down every diner he stopped at that had good pie. He loved cherry pie. Said the best he ever had was at this little place in upstate New York... 

But anyway, yeah. It's been months of this. Either I'm on top of the world or I'm ready to bite someone's head off. Just not myself at all.

Well, yeah, it's definitely been messing with things. At work, I've had to ask dispatch and some of my clients to repeat details that I normally would have tracked just fine. And I know a couple of my sales follow-ups totally slipped through the cracks because my brain was just chasing a hundred different ideas at once. It's like trying to catch leaves in a windstorm, you know? 

Speaking of windstorms, we had a bad one come through about a month ago. Knocked down that big old oak tree in the Henderson's front yard down the street. Barely missed their roof, but it completely crushed their mailbox. One of those fancy brick ones, too. Must have cost a fortune to put in. I remember when I built my own mailbox post out of some scrap cedar I had left over from fixing the deck. Cedar smells so good when you cut it. Reminds me of my grandfather's workshop. He used to carve these little wooden ducks... just sit there for hours with a pocket knife and a block of wood. He gave me one when I was seven, painted it to look like a mallard. Green head, little white ring around the neck. I think it's still in a box up in the attic somewhere. I really should go up there and clean that out. It's getting so cluttered you can hardly walk around up there...

But, uh, yeah. It's been tough at home, too. My wife and daughter, they've been walking on eggshells around me. I can just see it in their faces. I'm supposed to be the one helping out, keeping things steady, and instead, I know I'm just adding a whole lot of strain to the house.

I'm not talking about some time way back when. I'm talking about right now, these past months. It's just been this constant thing where I'm either completely fired up, like I'm invincible and everything's gonna be just fine no matter what, or I'm biting everyone's head off over the dumbest little things around the house or out working in the yard. And that's just not me. I'm not a guy who flies off the handle. \n\nSpeaking of handles, I actually need to fix the handle on my favorite shovel. The wood's starting to splinter near the grip. Doc, are you reading off a checklist or something? I swear we just went over this exact same thing. Like I keep telling you, it’s not just a week. I'm missing details I normally never, ever miss. I'll completely overlook a client’s delivery note, or I'll totally forget who I promised to call back. I've been driving for a long time and I don't make rookie mistakes like that, so... yeah, I figured I better come talk to somebody.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any periods where you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized that lasted for several days or more?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, actually. For months now, I've had these stretches where I'm just... I don't know, too fired up. Like I'm on top of the world and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what. Which is a nice feeling, I guess. Reminds me of when I first started driving, back in the late nineties. Man, the rigs were different then. Simpler. You didn't have all these computers telling you when to shift or tracking your every move. It was just you, the diesel, and the open road. I used to run this old Peterbilt, beautiful truck, cherry red... \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I get really upbeat. Or, on the flip side, I get super irritable. I'll be home off a run, and I'm just snapping over the smallest things. Like, my neighbor’s dog was barking the other day, and normally I love dogs—I had a Golden Retriever named Buster for twelve years, best dog you could ever ask for, he used to ride in the cab with me sometimes—but this barking just set me off. Or I'll get mad about something stupid in the yard. It’s just not my usual way, you know? I’m usually a pretty easygoing guy.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever had a time when you were so full of energy or ideas that you were doing things much more intensely than usual, like taking on big projects, talking more than usual, or feeling like you didn't need much sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, for sure. I just keep finding extra jobs for myself lately. Like, I’ll be up rewriting all my route plans to shave off a few miles, or I'm calling to check on the new drivers way after hours. And honestly, some of these new kids they’re hiring now... they don't know a wrench from a steering wheel. I swear, if they broke down outside of Barstow in the middle of July, they'd just sit in the cab and cry. Back when I started, you had to know how to actually fix your own rig. I remember this one time my radiator blew on Route 66, right near this old diner that made the absolute best cherry pie you've ever had in your life. The waitress there—I think her name was Betty, or maybe Dot?—she gave me a slice on the house while I waited for parts to come in from Flagstaff. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, I just get so antsy if I sit still for too long. If I try to watch TV, my skin just crawls. So if I'm not doing truck stuff, I'm fixing every little thing around the house. I completely took apart the lawnmower last week because the blades were getting dull, and you know, we've got that Bermuda grass in the front yard. If you don't stay on top of it, it just completely takes over the flower beds, and my wife just spent a fortune planting all these petunias... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, the energy. Yeah, I just can't stop moving.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you felt like you didn’t need much sleep at all, like you could go and go without really resting?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, that's exactly how it's been. Most nights I sleep maybe three or four hours, tops. Then I’m just... up. Wide awake before daylight, feeling completely ready to roll instead of worn out like I should be. \n\nUsually, I'm the kind of guy who needs a solid eight hours. If I don't get it, I'm dragging. Especially driving those long routes, you know? You get tired behind the wheel of an eighteen-wheeler, that's how people die. I remember this one guy I used to run with, Jimmy, he fell asleep at the wheel outside of Denver. Totalled his rig, took out a guardrail, and ended up in the hospital for a month. He was lucky he didn't kill anybody. After that, he quit driving and opened a hardware store. Good for him, honestly. Hardware stores are great. I love walking down the aisles, just looking at all the tools and the different kinds of screws and nails. You never know when you're gonna need a specific kind of bolt, right? I've got this whole organizer system in my garage... \n\nBut yeah, sleep. I just don't seem to need it lately. Three or four hours and I'm good to go.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when you were doing things so intensely—like working constantly, spending a lot of money, or getting very involved with projects or people—that others around you noticed and said something about it?\n\nPatient: Well, no, not really. I mean, I don't think anybody's said anything about me going overboard with projects or money or anything. I just do what needs doing, you know? Though, speaking of money, the price of diesel right now is just... it's out of control. I remember back in '98, you could fill up a whole rig and still have enough left over for a decent steak dinner. There was this place off I-40, Big Tex Steakhouse. They had this 72-ounce steak challenge. If you could eat the whole thing, plus the baked potato, the salad, the roll, and the shrimp cocktail in under an hour, it was free. I never tried it myself, I'm more of a chicken fried steak kind of guy, but I watched a buddy of mine, Carl, try it once. He got about halfway through the steak and just turned green. We had to pull over three times on the way to Albuquerque just so he could be sick. He never looked at a steak the same way again... The desert out there, it's just... it's something else. You get out past Van Horn and it's just you, the highway, and miles of nothing but scrub brush and rocks. I used to love those long hauls when I was younger. Just put on some Willie Nelson, roll the windows down, and let the miles eat themselves up. I actually saw Willie play once, back in '86, I think it was. Austin City Limits. He played for three hours straight, didn't even stop for a drink of water. Man, the way he plays that old guitar of his, Trigger, with the hole worn right through the wood... it's like the guitar is a part of him. I tried learning guitar once. Bought a cheap acoustic from a pawn shop, but my fingers are just too thick for the strings. Kept muting the chords. My buddy Dave, though, he could play anything with strings. Banjo, mandolin, you name it. He actually built his own banjo out of an old snare drum and a piece of maple he found in a scrap yard... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really noticed any specific week where things were different. It's just all kind of blended together since my daughter moved back.\n\nDoctor: Have there been any distinct times in the past when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a full week at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, like I keep trying to explain, it's not really just a week here or a week there in the past. It’s right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what happens, or I’m just snapping at everyone over the smallest little things around the house and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way, you know? I'm usually pretty level-headed. \n\nSpeaking of the yard, I finally got that John Deere running again yesterday. Turns out it wasn't just the carburetor flooding, the fuel line was cracked right near the filter. Had to drive all the way down to the hardware store on 4th Street to get a replacement hose. That store has been there forever. The guy who owns it, Mr. Abernathy, he must be eighty years old if he's a day. Still works the counter. He used to give me and my brother those little root beer barrels wrapped in cellophane when we'd go in there with my dad. My brother, he actually swallowed one whole once. Choked on it right there in aisle three next to the PVC pipes. Dad had to do the Heimlich maneuver on him. Scared the living daylights out of all of us. After that, my brother wouldn't touch hard candy for years. He actually became a dentist, believe it or not. Got a practice up in Boise... \n\nBut yeah, I haven't really had separate weeks in the past like you're asking. It's just been this whole long stretch for months where I'm either feeling on top of the world or getting ticked off over nothing.\n\nDoctor: Can you think back and tell me if there were any specific times when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a whole week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like a broken record here. Like I've been trying to tell you, it's not just one specific week in the past that I can point to. It's right now. For months now, I’ve just been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out perfectly no matter what, or I’m snapping at everyone over the smallest little things at home and out in the yard. And that really isn't my usual way. \n\nBut speaking of broken records, my daughter actually brought a real record player with her when she moved back in. Vinyl, she calls it. Takes up half the living room console. She's got all these old albums she bought at thrift stores... Fleetwood Mac, The Eagles. I used to have Hotel California on 8-track in my first rig. It was a Peterbilt 359, classic long hood. Man, I put a million miles on that truck. The sleeper cab was so small you couldn't even sit up straight in it, but she rode like an absolute dream on the interstate. I had this custom shifter knob made out of a real eight-ball that I won in a pool tournament down in Amarillo. The guy I beat was so mad he broke his cue over his knee right there in the bar... \n\nWait, what was the question again? Did you want to know about my daughter moving back in?\n\nDoctor: Can you think back to any time before these past seven months when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a full week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Look, Doc, I don't mean to be difficult, but I really don't know how else to say it. I'm not talking about some time way back when. I'm talking about right now, these past months. It's just been this constant thing where I'm either completely fired up, like I'm invincible and everything's gonna be just fine no matter what, or I'm biting everyone's head off over the dumbest little things around the house or out working in the yard. And that's just not me. I'm not a guy who flies off the handle. \n\nSpeaking of handles, I actually need to fix the handle on my favorite shovel. The wood's starting to splinter near the grip. I’ve had that shovel for fifteen years. Bought it at a garage sale from a guy who used to work for the city parks department. He had all kinds of crazy stories about what they’d find in the parks after dark. He told me once they found a whole living room set—couch, TV, coffee table, the works—just set up right in the middle of the softball field at Memorial Park. Said it looked like someone was just waiting to watch a game. Speaking of softball, my daughter used to play. She was a pitcher, had a mean fastball. We used to practice in the backyard until it got so dark we couldn't see the ball anymore. We went through so many softballs, losing them in the neighbor's hedges... \n\nAnyway, what was I saying? Oh, right. Yeah, it's just been these past months of feeling either way too up or just plain irritable. Not some specific week in the past.\n\nDoctor: Before these past seven months, can you recall any times in your life when you felt unusually upbeat, irritable, or energized for at least a full week, where you acted so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I swear we're going in circles here. I really can't think of any time before this recent stretch. It's just been these last seven months or so where I'm either feeling ten feet tall and bulletproof or ready to bite somebody's head off over nothing. Before that? Just my regular self, working and paying the bills. \n\nThough speaking of bills, I just got the electric bill yesterday, and I swear they must be reading the meter wrong. It was double what it was last month. I tried calling the utility company, but you know how that goes. You sit on hold for an hour listening to that terrible elevator music. It's always that same soft jazz, you know? Sounds like a saxophone played underwater. My buddy Earl used to play the sax in high school. He was actually pretty good, played in the marching band. They had these awful wool uniforms, maroon and gold. Earl passed out from the heat during the Fourth of July parade one year. Just fell right over into the tuba player. The tuba got a massive dent in it, and the school made Earl's parents pay for the repairs. Can you believe that? His dad was furious, threatened to sue the school board... \n\nBut anyway, no. Before these last few months, I don't remember any weeks where I was acting all crazy energized or irritable like you're talking about. Just this current stretch.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods lasting at least a week where your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and during which you were acting so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, I feel like I'm talking to a brick wall here. I keep telling you, yes! But it's not just one week. For months now, I've been completely wired, just way too fired up. Like I'm invincible and everything is going to work out perfectly, no matter what happens. Or, on the flip side, I'm snapping at everyone over the tiniest little things around the house or out in the yard. And that is not how I usually am. I'm usually a pretty even-keeled guy. \n\nSpeaking of the yard, I finally got around to fixing the carburetor on my riding mower. It's a John Deere, older model, green and yellow, you know the one. I had to order the part from this guy in Nebraska off the internet. Took three weeks to get here. The mail system is just a joke these days. I remember when my uncle worked for the post office back in the eighties. He drove one of those little white trucks with the steering wheel on the wrong side. He used to let me ride in the passenger side—well, the left side—when he did his rural route on Saturdays. We'd listen to the AM radio, mostly farm reports and old country music. George Jones, Merle Haggard. You don't hear music like that anymore. Now it's all just computers making noises... \n\nBut yeah, to answer your question, it's been months of feeling like this, not just a week. Too up or too irritable. Just not myself.\n\nDoctor: Have there been specific times in the past when your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and this lasted for at least a full week at a time?\n\nPatient: Doc, I... I don't know how many different ways I can say this. I feel like a broken record. Yes, my mood has been totally out of whack, but it's not some specific week in the past. For months now, I’ve been either way too fired up, like I'm completely unstoppable and everything is going to work out no matter what, or I’m just snapping at everybody over the smallest little things around the house or out in the yard. And that just isn’t my usual way. I'm a truck driver, I'm used to just rolling with the punches. \n\nYou know, speaking of rolling, I had a blowout on my rig about three weeks ago on I-80. Rear passenger side tire just shredded. Sounded like a shotgun going off. Scared the living daylights out of this guy in a little silver Honda next to me. He swerved so hard I thought he was going into the ditch. I managed to limp it over to the shoulder, but let me tell you, changing a tire on a semi on the side of the interstate with semis blowing past you at seventy miles an hour... it's enough to rattle your teeth. The wind coming off those trucks just hits you like a physical wall. My dad used to drive a rig back in the seventies, long before all the electronic logs and GPS stuff. He used to keep a little notebook on the dash and write down every diner he stopped at that had good pie. He loved cherry pie. Said the best he ever had was at this little place in upstate New York... \n\nBut anyway, yeah. It's been months of this. Either I'm on top of the world or I'm ready to bite someone's head off. Just not myself at all.\n\nDoctor: Have there been times when your mood was so high or so irritable that it clearly interfered with your ability to function in your daily life, like at work or at home, even for just a few days at a time?\n\nPatient: Well, yeah, it's definitely been messing with things. At work, I've had to ask dispatch and some of my clients to repeat details that I normally would have tracked just fine. And I know a couple of my sales follow-ups totally slipped through the cracks because my brain was just chasing a hundred different ideas at once. It's like trying to catch leaves in a windstorm, you know? \n\nSpeaking of windstorms, we had a bad one come through about a month ago. Knocked down that big old oak tree in the Henderson's front yard down the street. Barely missed their roof, but it completely crushed their mailbox. One of those fancy brick ones, too. Must have cost a fortune to put in. I remember when I built my own mailbox post out of some scrap cedar I had left over from fixing the deck. Cedar smells so good when you cut it. Reminds me of my grandfather's workshop. He used to carve these little wooden ducks... just sit there for hours with a pocket knife and a block of wood. He gave me one when I was seven, painted it to look like a mallard. Green head, little white ring around the neck. I think it's still in a box up in the attic somewhere. I really should go up there and clean that out. It's getting so cluttered you can hardly walk around up there...\n\nBut, uh, yeah. It's been tough at home, too. My wife and daughter, they've been walking on eggshells around me. I can just see it in their faces. I'm supposed to be the one helping out, keeping things steady, and instead, I know I'm just adding a whole lot of strain to the house.\n\nDoctor: Have you had any distinct periods that lasted at least a full week where your mood was so high or so irritable that it was clearly different from your usual self, and during which you were acting so differently that someone close to you might have noticed?\n\nPatient: Doc, are you reading off a checklist or something? I swear we just went over this exact same thing. Like I keep telling you, it’s not just a week. For months now, I’ve been either way too fired up, feeling like absolutely everything is going to work out no matter what, or I'm just snapping over the smallest stuff at home and out in the yard. And that just isn't my usual way. \n\nLike the other day, I lost my temper just because the garden hose was left uncoiled. Just a stupid hose. Speaking of which, I really need to get one of those heavy-duty black rubber ones for washing down the rig. The cheap green vinyl ones always kink up on you when the temperature drops. We had a cold snap back in, oh, must have been '98, that froze the wash lines solid at the old terminal. Me and this guy Tommy—we called him Two-Ton Tommy because he used to haul heavy equipment out of the limestone quarries—we actually had to use blowtorches to thaw the pipes out so we could work. Tommy was a real character, always wore these bright red suspenders and chewed that cheap bubblegum, the kind that came with the little comic strips inside. Bazooka, I think it was called? You'd chew it for five minutes and it would turn into a solid rock in your mouth. He used to stick it to the dashboard of his Peterbilt... \n\nBut yeah, I don't know why you keep asking about a week. It's been months of me being too revved up or just plain irritable."
